(needs_source: the report of the Prophet's wudu and his saying that whoever adds or subtracts has done wrong is given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:40:30}
- He says the essential pillar of the full bath is covering the whole body with water, and that intention is needed, since a person bathing to cool off after work is not bathing to remove the greater impurity. He recalls Malik's example of a person in a state of impurity who slips into a stream, says the Prophet began by washing the private parts, and advises making sure water reaches the roots of the hair. See [[practice/ghusl-al-janaba|the bath of purification]] and [[concept/niyya|intention]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report that the Prophet washed his private parts first when bathing is quoted without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:43:00}
- He says the water used for purification must have no colour, taste or smell, so soap added to the vessel spoils it, and that scholars call water that has run off the body used water. He adds that the Muslim looks for the secret in worship. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:47:30}
- He lists covering the nakedness as a condition, saying people look at a person's outward and he should be mindful before God, and then facing the qibla, which he says is the body turned toward the Kaaba while the heart looks to its Creator. See [[concept/awra|nakedness]] and [[concept/qibla|the qibla]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:50:30}
- He quotes a saying that God may open the door of worship and close the door of acceptance, and that a sin that brings humility may be better than a deed that brings pride. On the opening takbir he says the tongue says God is greater while the mind may be on markets, work and money, so the heart should be made to agree with the tongue. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that God may open a door of worship and close the door of acceptance is quoted without naming its author) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:53:00}
- He says the prayer continues with seeking refuge in God from the devil, whom he calls the true enemy, and then the basmala, quoting the saying that any work not begun with God's name is lacking in good. See [[passage/quran-1-1|al-Fatiha, verse 1]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that any work not begun with God's name is lacking is quoted by sense without narrator) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:55:00}
- He explains the opening chapter by meaning: that God is Lord of all the worlds and so the only one to rely on; that the Merciful is wider than the Compassionate, mercy in this world reaching believer and unbeliever alike and mercy in the next reserved for the upright; and that the Master of the Day of Judgment is the only one to fear and hope in. See [[passage/quran-1-2|al-Fatiha, verse 2]], [[passage/quran-1-3|al-Fatiha, verse 3]] and [[passage/quran-1-4|al-Fatiha, verse 4]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:57:30}
- He says the servant who recites the verse of worship is saying he worships and affirms none but God, and cites a divine saying in which God tells the son of Adam that He created things for him and created him for Himself. See [[passage/quran-1-5|al-Fatiha, verse 5]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the divine saying in which God addresses the son of Adam about creating things for him and him for God is given without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 00:59:00}
- He says the straight path is the shortest line between two points, and recalls a report of the Prophet drawing a straight line with side paths, a devil at the head of each. On the path of those favoured, he names prophets such as Noah, Abraham, Idris, David and Solomon, and as examples of the righteous Abu Bakr, Umar, Uthman, Ali, al-Hasan al-Basri, Abu Hanifa, Malik, al-Shafii and Ahmad, and not those who earned anger. See [[passage/quran-1-6|al-Fatiha, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-1-7|al-Fatiha, verse 7]], [[concept/sirat|the path]], [[person/nuh|Noah]], [[person/ibrahim|Abraham]], [[person/idris|Idris]], [[person/dawud|David]], [[person/sulayman|Solomon]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]], [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]], [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]], [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]], [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik]], [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafii]] and [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the Prophet drawing a line with side paths and a devil at each is garbled and has no narrator) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:07:30}
- Returning to the hadith, he says the man was told only the five prayers, the zakat and Ramadan and the answer to each was nothing more unless he volunteered, while the voluntary prayers and extra zakat would have brought more good. He says the versions differ in wording, one reporting it as Muslim does and another as al-Bukhari does, that the man is among the people of Paradise, and that voluntary acts complete the obligatory ones like topping up a bank account. See [[work/sahih-muslim|Sahih Muslim]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:17:00}
- He relates from Umar the hadith in which a man in very white clothes with very black hair, with no sign of travel and known to none of them, sat before the Prophet and asked about Islam, faith, ihsan and the Hour. The Prophet answered that faith is belief in God, the last day and the decree, good and bad, among other things he lists; that ihsan is to worship God as if you see Him; that the one asked knows no more than the asker of the Hour; and that its signs include the barefoot, the poor and the shepherds competing in building and the loss of trust. Afterwards he said it was Jibril who came to teach them their religion. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], [[person/jibril|Jibril]], [[concept/iman|faith]], [[concept/ihsan|ihsan]] and [[concept/qadar|the decree]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:20:30}
- He says the Companions answered that God and His Messenger know best, and that he draws a rule from it: in matters of religion one says God and His Messenger know best, while in worldly matters one says God knows best. He reads the loss of trust as handing an office to someone unfit for it. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep14 01:22:00}

## Episode 15: insulting a Muslim is wickedness and fighting him is disbelief
- He says the hadith of the day concerns rights that slip from people's minds in the press of life, that knowing them keeps a person from taking more than his due and lets him pay what he owes his brothers, and that the gate of wrongs, which is the rights of people, is the hardest gate to cross on the Day of Judgement. See [[concept/huquq-al-insan|human rights]] and [[concept/mazalim|wrongs]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:05:00}
- He says people dislike the word of truth, that whoever dislikes the truth dislikes God since God is the Truth, and that a person who is advised against his wishes avoids the adviser's company. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:05:30}
- He says the hadith has five words, that he takes a sample from each book of Sahih al-Bukhari and is still in the book of faith, and that knowledge without faith does not benefit. See [[concept/iman|faith]] and [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:07:00}
- He tells a story of a space launch whose officials assured everyone that no fault could ever happen, to show that knowledge without faith does not protect. (needs_source: the story is told without names or reference and the details are garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:08:30}
- He says the words of God are of two kinds, creative and legislative, and cites the verse on the trees of the earth as pens and the sea as ink to show that God's words do not run out. See [[passage/quran-31-27|Luqman, verse 27]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:10:00}
- He says the first creative word was be, and Adam came to be, that the angels said God have mercy on you when he sneezed so mercy was the first word Adam heard, and that Eve was created from him, citing the verse that He made for you spouses from yourselves to find rest in them and set between you affection and mercy. See [[person/adam|Adam]], [[person/hawwa|Eve]] and [[passage/quran-30-21|al-Rum, verse 21]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the angels' words to Adam has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:12:00}
- He says there are matters beyond the reach of human reason, such as Jesus coming from a mother without a father, and that Mary wished she had died before this and then answered that her provision was from God. See [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]], [[person/maryam|Mary]], [[passage/quran-19-23|Maryam, verse 23]] and [[passage/quran-3-37|Al Imran, verse 37]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:13:00}
- He retells the prayer of Zakariya, citing the verse on bones growing weak and the head shining white, his astonishment that he would have a son, and the sign that he would not speak to people for three days, and says the ages of Zakariya and his wife are given in the reliable books of tafsir. See [[person/zakariyya|Zakariya]], [[person/yahya|Yahya]], [[passage/quran-19-4|Maryam, verse 4]], [[passage/quran-19-8|Maryam, verse 8]], [[passage/quran-3-41|Al Imran, verse 41]] and [[passage/quran-19-10|Maryam, verse 10]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the ages of Zakariya and his wife are not clear in the recording and not referenced) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:15:30}
- He says knowledge is of four kinds, that the first and beneficial one is knowledge of God, His book and His remembrance, and that there is also harmful knowledge. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]] and [[concept/dhikr|remembrance]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the four kinds are not itemised in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:19:30}
- He says the believer fears that his deeds will be nullified without his noticing, and that the first cause in the book of God is raising one's voice above the voice of the Prophet, citing the verse that your deeds may be nullified while you do not perceive, and says a person who answers a command by saying she is not convinced is in effect raising her voice over him. See [[passage/quran-49-2|al-Hujurat, verse 2]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:23:00}
- He says that rejecting part of the book is rejecting all of it, and contrasts this with a person who commits a major sin while feeling guilt over it and hoping for repentance. This is his view. (needs_source: the second half of the distinction is garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:24:00}
- He says that wronging people also nullifies deeds, citing that you are the best community brought out for people, enjoining the right, forbidding the wrong and believing in God, and that faith must be complete, and mentions that the supplication of one wronged rises at night while the wrongdoer sleeps. See [[passage/quran-3-110|Al Imran, verse 110]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the remark on the supplication of the wronged is not attributed or referenced) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:26:30}
- He urges repentance and says the rights of people are not forgiven by repentance to God alone, so a person should review his account, settle what he owes and not add a new wrong, and asks God to forgive past mistakes. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:30:00}
- He quotes a saying of a pious predecessor that he never measured his words against his deeds without fearing he was lying, and describes another who treated each night as the night of his last prayer, recitation and remembrance, then relates that one of them heard a recitation about the truthful being asked of their truthfulness, wept and said what then of the liars. See [[passage/quran-33-8|al-Ahzab, verse 8]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the names are garbled in the recording and the sayings have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:31:30}
- He relates from Ibn Abi Mulayka, whom he calls a successor, that he met thirty Companions of the Prophet, each fearing hypocrisy for himself, and from al-Hasan al-Basri that only a believer fears it and only a hypocrite feels safe from it, and says that feeling secure is a sign of hypocrisy. See [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] and [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both reports are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:33:30}
- He relates from Abdullah ibn Masud that the Prophet said insulting a Muslim is wickedness and fighting him is disbelief, and says a society that applied this hadith would be the greatest in history and resemble the society of the Companions. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Masud]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the chain read before the hadith is garbled and no collection is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:36:00}
- He says he has gathered the rights of a Muslim over his brother from the hadith and the Qur'an, counting forty-two, and that he will treat ten of them in this lesson. See [[concept/huquq-al-muslim-ala-akhihi|the rights of a Muslim over his brother]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the forty-two rights are not itemised in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:19:00}
- As the first right he names the greeting, saying peace is a name of God and a name of Paradise, and that the greeting is rewarded with rising numbers of good deeds when completed with God's mercy and answered. See [[practice/greeting-salam|the greeting of peace]] and [[concept/asma-al-salam|the name al-Salam]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the counts of good deeds are garbled in the recording and given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:38:00}
- He relates that the Prophet greeted Sad ibn Muadh and Sad did not answer the first time, and says peace descends on Sad and his household, and mentions by sense the hadith on spreading the greeting among you and that one sign of the Hour is greeting only those one knows. See [[person/sad-ibn-muadh|Sad ibn Muadh]]. (needs_source: the report of Sad is garbled in the recording and both hadiths by sense have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:40:30}
- As the second right he says to love for one's brother what one loves for oneself, mentioning by sense that this is the first degree of faith, and he laments those who love evil for others and asks God to make Muslims love each other. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:47:00}
- As the third right he names not harming a Muslim by word or by attacking his honour, and tells a story of a rival who plotted against a king's companion by means of a letter to show the saying that whoever digs a pit for his brother falls into it. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is told without names or reference and is garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:51:00}
- He names humility toward every Muslim as another right, relating that the Prophet said God commanded humility so that none boasts over another, and says Muslim reports it, then mentions by sense that the people of Paradise are three kinds, among them the merciful tender-hearted man and the chaste one with dependants, and that the people of Hell are five, among them the treacherous and the liar. See [[concept/tawadu|humility]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith has no chain and the list is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:55:30}
- As another right he says not to listen to reports people carry about each other, relating that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz allowed no backbiting in his presence, and mentions by sense that the Prophet said do not report to me anything bad of my Companions, for he loved to meet them with a sound heart. See [[concept/namimah|tale-bearing]] and [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of Umar and the hadith by sense have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:00:30}
- He says it is not lawful to forsake a brother beyond three days, mentioning by sense that the better of the two is the one who begins with the greeting, and that whoever does not accept his brother's apology, true or false, will not smell the fragrance of Paradise. This is his view. (needs_source: both hadiths are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:08:00}
- He names excellence in dealing with everyone as a right, saying God has prescribed excellence in everything, and mentions by sense a report of a man slaughtering a sheep in front of its mother who was told, is there no mercy in your heart, do you want to kill it twice. See [[concept/ihsan|excellence]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the sheep is quoted by sense without attribution or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:10:00}
- He names seeking permission before entering a brother's home as a right, and says to accept people's excuses, citing the advice to find seventy excuses for a brother, since he may have an excuse one does not know. See [[concept/istidhan|seeking permission]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about seventy excuses is given without an attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:12:00}
- He relates that Umar ibn al-Khattab found people doing wrong inside a house and one of them told him they had disobeyed God in one matter and he had disobeyed in three, among them spying and entering the house without asking permission, and that Umar accepted the man's repentance. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the end is garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:14:30}
- He ends with good character as a right, citing the verse that the Prophet was of great character, mentioning by sense that those nearest the Prophet in the highest Paradise are the best in character and that the believer is one who is friendly and befriended, and that a Muslim should honour the elder, show mercy to the young and know the scholar's due, and says these are ten rights of a Muslim over his brother and that the lesson continues next time. See [[concept/akhlaq|character]] and [[passage/quran-68-4|al-Qalam, verse 4]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadiths are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep15 01:19:00}

## Episode 16: reviling a Muslim, the witnesses against a person and the rights of Muslims
- He continues from the previous lesson the hadith that he relates from [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Mas'ud]] that the Prophet said reviling a Muslim is wickedness and fighting him is disbelief, and says he has gathered from sound hadiths and the Qur'an some twenty-four duties that Muslims owe one another. See [[concept/huquq-al-muslim-ala-akhihi|the rights of a Muslim over his brother]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is read in garbled form with no collection named, and the count of twenty-four is his own compilation) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:03:00}
- He says the disaster is that words move hearts inside the mosque but are not applied outside it, and that worship itself is in doubt when it does not restrain, saying by sense that prayer is of no good to one whose hand, eye, ear, tongue or foot goes to what God has forbidden. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on prayer is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:04:00}
- He says scholars have gathered that ten witnesses will testify against a person on the Day of Judgement, and begins with the first of them, the earth one lives on. See [[concept/yawm-al-qiyama|the Day of Resurrection]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the count of ten is given as the gathering of unnamed scholars, with no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:05:00}
- He quotes by sense a saying to guard against the earth, the mother of people, without naming who said it at this point, since nobody does anything on its back, good or bad, but it will report it, and that a person's whole life is lived on it, so its report comes first. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain or a named speaker) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:06:30}
- He relates by sense a report that the earth will say to a believer laid in it that he was the most beloved of God's creatures to it while he walked on its back, so his grave is opened wide as a garden of Paradise, whereas for another the grave presses on him until his ribs overlap. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:08:30}
- He says each person, literate or not and in whatever language, will be brought his own book and made to read it, and refers to the verse that one's own self suffices as reckoner that day. See [[passage/quran-17-14|al-Isra, verse 14]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:08:00}
- He says every deed has a place it comes from and a place it rises to, and that permissible acts are in themselves neither good nor evil but can turn into one or the other by how they are done, giving the example of food. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:10:00}
- He says the believer has a place on earth that he fills with worship and a place in heaven where his deeds rise, whereas of a wicked person who dies it is said that praise is due to God who relieved the land and its people of him. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying has no chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:12:00}
- He turns to night and day, describing how they follow one another from sunrise through the afternoon to sunset, and says this alternation points to the One who turns hearts. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:14:00}
- He relates that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] told [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] that God has a deed of the night He does not accept by day and a deed of the day He does not accept by night, and illustrates it with deeds tied to their times such as fasting and prayer. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain and the examples are partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:15:00}
- He says the Companions rejoiced when night came because they rose in prayer, referring to the verse about their sides leaving their beds to call on their Lord in fear and hope, and that fear comes first, then hope in God's mercy. See [[passage/quran-32-16|al-Sajda, verse 16]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:16:30}
- He says the limbs will testify against a person, and relates a report, with no speaker or source named, of a servant who disputes what is recorded and says he accepts no witness against himself but himself, so God seals his mouth and his limbs speak, each telling what it did, after which the servant says his sins are greater still and God answers that He knows them better. This is his view. (needs_source: the report has no chain or collection named, and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:24:30}
- He says the two recording angels write down each deed, and gives the example of a man of sixty who counts some twenty-one thousand five hundred days, saying a single sin a day would leave that many on his record, and adds that earth, sky, angels and limbs all witness while God is the best of witnesses. This is his view. (needs_source: the man's reckoning is told without a chain and the figures are approximate in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:28:30}
- He turns to the rights themselves, saying the first is mutual mercy and the second that one loves for his brother what he loves for himself, and that these rights are in practice not found among people. See [[concept/huquq-al-muslim-ala-akhihi|the rights of a Muslim over his brother]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on loving for one's brother is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:31:00}
- He lists further rights: not to look down on any Muslim in knowledge or anything else, not to listen to what people carry about one another, not to forsake a brother beyond three, not to enter on him without permission, to treat him with good character, to respect elders, to be gentle with the young and to know the scholars' due. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:32:30}
- He gives as another right meeting one's brother with a smiling face, and relates by sense that the Prophet in his home was among his family and that a woman, not named at this point in the recording, said that when prayer time came he seemed not to know them, advising husbands to say kind words to their wives and not to spread each other's faults outside. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense with no named speaker and no collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:35:30}
- He tells that the Prophet, in Mecca around the age of thirty, was promised a meeting at the Kaaba by a man, and came to the same place at the same time each day for several days, offered as a lesson in keeping a promise. This is his view. (needs_source: the story has no chain, and its details are partly garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:39:30}
- He gives as the twelfth right that a person gives people their due from himself, and relates that when [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar's]] wife sent a gift to the wife of the Byzantine ruler, who sent jewels in return, Umar put the return into the Muslims' treasury and gave her only what matched her own gift, as an example of fairness even toward one's family. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of the gift has no chain and the wife is not named in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:45:30}
- He says the Prophet gave people their stations and relates that when the noble [[person/adi-ibn-hatim|Adi ibn Hatim]] entered, the Prophet gave him his own cloak to sit on, and quotes the saying that only those of merit know the merit of the people of merit. This is his view. (needs_source: the report and the closing saying have no chain, and the telling is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:51:30}
- He says that when rulers and scholars are sound all people are sound and when they are corrupted all are corrupted, quoting an imam whose name is unclear in the recording as comparing scholars to the salt of the land, which spoils it once it spoils, and warns against compromising with wrong. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without a source and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 00:57:00}
- He says people should not be set against one another and that reconciling between people, beginning within one's own household, is among the greatest matters, advising a husband to assume the best of a wife who may be tired or troubled by a family matter. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:02:00}
- He says whoever shields a Muslim's faults is shielded by God in this world and the next, and that shaming a brother over a sin risks being afflicted with it, so one should pray for his guidance rather than curse him. This is his view. (needs_source: both sayings are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:03:00}
- He relates by sense a saying of the Prophet to be chaste so that one's women are kept chaste, says whoever violates the honour of others will find his own household violated, and advises protecting people's honour. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:05:30}
- He turns to the design of the human body, saying the hair and mucus of the nose and the spiralling passages filter and warm the air, so that air at fifty below zero reaches the lungs at about thirty-seven degrees, and refers to the verse that this is God's creation, so show what others have created. See [[passage/quran-31-11|Luqman, verse 11]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the temperatures are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:14:00}
- He says that when a sneezer praises God one answers may God have mercy on you, and lists other reported wordings for the sneezer's reply, such as may God have mercy on us and on you. See [[concept/tashmit-replying-to-sneezing|replying to a sneeze]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the variant wordings are listed without a chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:15:00}
- He relates by sense a report that one who visits a sick person is covered in mercy, advises not to linger long with the patient, and counts among the rights following funerals and, for men, visiting graves, adding that in the majority view of jurists visiting graves is not for women. See [[practice/ziyarat-al-qubur|visiting graves]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on the visitor is quoted by sense without a chain and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:17:30}
- He sums up that these are some of the rights of Muslims and recalls by sense the hadith that reviling a Muslim is wickedness and fighting him is disbelief, so one should not insult, fight or spread rumours about a Muslim. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep16 01:17:30}

## Episode 17: the lawful is clear and the unlawful is clear
- He says today's hadith is one of the four that Ibn Hajar names near the start of Fath al-Bari as the pillars on which the religion stands, and he lists them: actions by intentions, the lawful is clear, whoever is wise leaves what does not concern him, and a fourth he names by its opening about who is nearest to the Prophet on the Day of Resurrection, adding that scholars differ over the exact list. See [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar]], [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]] and [[concept/niyya|intention]]. (needs_source: Ibn Hajar's selection of four hadiths is stated without a page; the list is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:04:00}
- He relates from al-Nu'man ibn Bashir that the Prophet said the lawful is clear and the unlawful is clear, and between them are doubtful matters that many people do not know; whoever avoids the doubtful clears his religion, and whoever falls into them is like a shepherd grazing around a protected pasture who is about to enter it; every king has a protected area and God's protected area is what He has forbidden; and in the body is a morsel which, if sound, the whole body is sound, and if corrupt, the whole body is corrupt, and it is the heart. He says al-Bukhari reports it. See [[person/al-numan-ibn-bashir|al-Nu'man ibn Bashir]], [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]], [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]] and [[concept/shubha-doubt|doubtful matters]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:05:00}
- He says the lawful and unlawful the hadith means are what God and His Messenger have declared so, and that Muslims do not legislate from themselves; the matter of the lawful and the unlawful, he says, must be taken from God. This is his view. See [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:05:30}
- He says he will first cover twelve general points on the lawful and the unlawful as an introduction, and he says that in this world there are two paths, the lawful and the unlawful, and in the hereafter two places, the garden or the fire; the lawful leads to the garden and the unlawful to the fire, and the doubtful matters are dangerous because a person who gets used to them drifts toward the unlawful. This is his view. See [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]] and [[concept/shubha-doubt|doubtful matters]]. (needs_source: the number of points, twelve, is announced without a full list) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:09:30}
- He says the Prophet is the most compassionate of people toward his community after God, and he tells that God asked him what would please him and he answered that he would not be pleased while any of his community was in the fire, whereupon God told him that whoever says there is no god but God will not remain forever in the fire. This is his view. (needs_source: report of the Prophet's exchange with God quoted without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:08:30}
- He says that what leads to the unlawful is itself unlawful, and gives the example of adultery, where a person is told not to approach it, so that its preliminaries, the glance, the touch and indecent words, are unlawful too. This is his view. See [[concept/zina|adultery]] and [[passage/quran-17-32|al-Isra, verse 32]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:10:30}
- He says the lawful stays lawful in God's book until the Day of Resurrection and the unlawful stays unlawful, so that what was lawful in a given year does not become unlawful decades later; when people say the world has changed, he says the world has not, since days, months and the sun and moon are as they were, with the count of months twelve in God's book, and what changes is how close people are to the Book and the Sunna. This is his view. See [[passage/quran-9-36|al-Tawba, verse 36]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:15:30}
- He holds that whoever declares lawful what God forbade, or forbids what God made lawful, has set up a partner with God, because he makes himself a god beside God, since permitting and forbidding belong to God alone. This is his view. See [[concept/shirk|associating partners with God]] and [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:16:30}
- He says that in his opinion whoever rejects a matter of the religion known by necessity, clear in the Qur'an or the Sunna, has left the faith, and he turns to the scholars' difference over someone who abandons the prayer: he says Ahmad ibn Hanbal took the plain sense of the saying that what separates us from them is the prayer, so whoever leaves it has disbelieved, and held him a disbeliever, while the majority, among them Abu Hanifa and al-Shafi'i, held that it is a disbelief that earns punishment in the fire for a time and then he leaves it, and he presents the difference among scholars as a mercy. This is his view. See [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]], [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]], [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]] and [[practice/salah|prayer]]. (needs_source: hadith on abandoning the prayer given by sense without narrator or collection; the other imams' names are partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:20:30}
- He says scholars each took what God opened to them, giving the face veil as an example: for the imam who takes the saying that the woman is an 'awra, the veil becomes obligatory; he says the one who follows evidence and the one who follows opinion are both to be respected, but the lawful and the unlawful must be observed as God and His Messenger commanded. This is his view. (needs_source: saying that the woman is an 'awra given by sense without chain; the imam he means is not named clearly in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:21:00}
- He says the lawful has degrees, some of it more lawful than other, and the unlawful has degrees, some of it worse than other; he gives the example of a government employee whose pay includes a small share, he says one or two percent, drawn from questionable taxes, set against a man who steals a thousand pounds from a rich man, and he tells of a man whose hand had a vein that would throb whenever he reached toward something doubtful, whom the transcript names as Abdullah. This is his view. See [[concept/wara|scrupulousness]]. (needs_source: percentages and the sum are stated without source; the story of the man with the vein is told without chain and the name is uncertain in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:35:00}
- He relates by sense a saying that God set obligations so they should not be neglected, set limits so they should not be overstepped, forbade things so they should not be violated, and kept silent about other things out of mercy and not forgetfulness, so people should not pry into them; he also says the worst of people before God is the one who keeps asking about something until it is made unlawful for people, and he cites the verse about not asking about things. This is his view. See [[passage/quran-5-101|al-Ma'ida, verse 101]]. (needs_source: hadith of obligations, limits and silence quoted by sense without narrator or collection; the second saying is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:37:00}
- He tells a story of a girl in the time of a commander of the believers who refused to cheat in selling milk, saying that if the commander of the believers does not see us then the Lord of the commander of the believers sees us, and he links the story to Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz; the names and descent are garbled in the transcript. See [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]. (needs_source: story of the girl and the milk told without chain; names and the line of descent are garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:45:00}
- He says that changing names does not change rulings: bank interest renamed as a return, wine called spirits, and immodest entertainment called culture remain what they are, and a Muslim who fears God cannot rename what God forbade; he also warns against evading the command of the lawful so as to make it unlawful. This is his view. See [[concept/riba|usury]] and [[concept/khamr|wine]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:49:00}
- He describes the pre-Islamic Arab practice of reading omens from birds before a journey, where a bird flying from right to left was a good sign and one from left to right a bad sign, and a raven was taken as a warning, This is his view. See [[concept/tatayyur-omens|omens]]. (needs_source: description of pre-Islamic Arab practice given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:51:00}
- He says that the prayer for guidance in a decision, istikhara, is not a matter of waiting for a dream; a person asks God for the best, then uses his reason and goes ahead, and if the matter is made easy he proceeds, and he says there is no need to repeat it again and again; he gives marriage as the example and says that in his own case he prayed it about marriage. This is his view. See [[practice/istikhara|istikhara]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:53:30}
- He says that something to do with looking into one's fortune is forbidden, the act itself not being clear in the transcript, and relates by sense that whoever does it believing in it has disbelieved in what was sent down on the Messenger, and whoever does it without believing has no work accepted from him for forty days. This is his view. (needs_source: hadith on believing in this act given by sense without narrator or collection; the act meant is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:54:30}
- He says God put a lawful substitute in place of each unlawful thing, trade in place of usury and thousands of other lawful drinks in place of wine, and he mentions by sense a hadith on wine that counts its seller, its carrier and others, saying they share the sin equally. This is his view. See [[concept/riba|usury]] and [[concept/khamr|wine]]. (needs_source: the hadith on those involved with wine is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:56:30}
- He lists the few forbidden foods, carrion, blood, pig's flesh, what is dedicated to other than God, and animals that are strangled, beaten, fallen, gored or part-eaten by a beast, and says everything beyond them is lawful; he says the same of clothing, where silk and gold are forbidden and linen, cotton, wool and the rest are lawful, and he says the default is permission unless a text forbids. This is his view. See [[passage/quran-5-3|al-Ma'ida, verse 3]] and [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:58:00}
- He says God forbade the worship of idols and that worship in this sense is not only bowing to stones but devotion to a wife, children, money, status or rank; he quotes God by sense as saying that He created the person for Himself and created things for the person's sake, so that one should not exhaust oneself with complaint. This is his view. See [[concept/shirk|associating partners with God]]. (needs_source: the divine saying about creating the person for Himself is quoted by sense without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 00:59:00}
- He says that in disputes over details of a mosque, one example being a feature some want to demolish as unlawful, which the transcript leaves unclear, people should first be taught the lawful, the unlawful and the fundamentals; he says a mosque should be plain, as the Prophet commanded, with no verses, ornament or inscription, and he relates that Umar told a builder to shelter people from heat and rain but not to paint it red or yellow. This is his view. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the saying of Umar to the builder is quoted without chain; the disputed mosque feature is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:01:00}
- He quotes Ali as saying that the lawful of this world carries a veil, its unlawful a punishment and its doubtful matters a reproach. See [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]] and [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. (needs_source: saying of Ali given without chain; the first term is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:03:00}
- He says that the rule that necessities permit what is forbidden is often misused; he relates by sense that God did not place the cure of this community in what He forbade, and he gives the case of a man lost in the desert with no food or water who may eat carrion, but only as much as keeps him alive. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith that God placed no cure in what He forbade is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:07:00}
- He says jurists hold that the meat of an animal fed on filth becomes impure until it is kept from that feed, so a chicken is to be kept on clean feed for three days and a sheep for seven, and he advises that a person who buys such a chicken keep it for those days before eating it. This is his view. (needs_source: the jurists' rule on animals that feed on filth and its three and seven day periods is stated without a named source; the period for cattle is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:11:00}
- He returns to the end of the hadith and explains the protected area with the image of a person's home: every king has a protected area and God's is what He has forbidden, so one stays far from the boundary as one keeps well back from the gate of a house, and the heart is the morsel that makes the body sound; he says a sound heart is free of deception, speaking about people's honour, prying into people's homes and secrets, and that God sends calm and light into such a heart. This is his view. See [[concept/hima|the protected area]] and [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep17 01:13:00}

## Episode 18: six reasons that close the door of success, the three kinds of servants and the Qur'an
- He opens by saying this is the fifth lesson on the hadith that faith has some sixty-odd branches and that modesty is a branch of faith, after four lessons on the branches, the narrations and the barriers that keep faith from a servant's heart, and he takes from Ibn al-Qayyim a point about the closing of the door of divine success on Muslims who know right from wrong. He says the lawful is clear and the unlawful is clear, asks what makes a Muslim commit sins when everyone knows right from wrong, and says that when a person hesitates over a matter his heart is not at rest with it, while the lawful is what the heart is at ease with. See [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]] and [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the point of Ibn al-Qayyim is relayed without a reference, and the clear lawful and unlawful and the heart's rest are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:03:30}
- He lists six reasons that close the door of divine success, and gives the first as being busy with the blessing instead of the Giver, picturing a person absorbed in companies, contracts and offices who never stops to ask who gave him all this. He tells of a man sitting with al-Hasan al-Basri with fruit before him who did not eat while he thought how to thank God for it, and he says one should name God at the start of eating and praise Him at the end, giving by sense a saying of the Prophet about naming God at the beginning and end of the meal, and a story that Abraham would not eat except at a price, whose price was naming God first and praising Him last. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]], [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] and [[person/ibrahim|Abraham]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of al-Hasan and the story of Abraham are given without a chain, and the saying of the Prophet is quoted by sense without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:06:30}
- He tells that a man told Ibn Sirin he had dreamed he was calling the call to prayer and Ibn Sirin said he would perform the pilgrimage, while to a second man with the same dream he said he would be accused of theft, explaining that the first dream matched the verse that commands announcing the pilgrimage to people and the second the verse in which the caravan is accused of theft. He also reads the account of Joseph's cup, saying the brothers answered that the penalty is for the one in whose saddlebag it is found, and that the law then followed made the thief a servant. See [[person/yusuf|Joseph]], [[passage/quran-22-27|al-Hajj, verse 27]], [[passage/quran-12-70|Yusuf, verse 70]] and [[passage/quran-12-75|Yusuf, verse 75]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the dream story of Ibn Sirin is told without a chain, and the claim about the law then in force is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:12:00}
- He says blessings are of three kinds: a blessing one knows and sometimes thanks God for and sometimes does not, such as health and security, a blessing one awaits, and a blessing one is in without knowing it until it is lost, adding that health is known only to the sick and wealth only to the poor. See [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:15:30}
- He relates that Umar stood on the pulpit one Friday and said he had heard the Prophet say, by sense, that praise belongs to God who made our wrongdoer forgiven, our moderate one bound for paradise and our forerunner a forerunner, and he ties it to a verse that divides the heirs of the Book into three kinds: the self-wronger, the middle one and the foremost in good deeds. He tells a report of al-Hasan al-Basri on those who go ahead and those who follow far behind, and says the forerunner of this community is a forerunner on the Day of Resurrection. See [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] and [[passage/quran-35-32|Fatir, verse 32]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying heard by Umar is quoted by sense without a collection, and the report of al-Hasan is garbled and given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:24:00}
- He relates that Gabriel came to the Prophet and asked whether he knew what the generous pardoner means, and said it is the one who replaces bad deeds with good deeds; he explains that one who pardons may keep the fault in mind, while the generous pardoner holds it and then does good to the person, and he says the repentant is beloved to the Merciful. He relates that when the Prophet feared for his community there came down a verse promising people their Lord's forgiveness even with their wrongdoing. See [[person/jibril|Gabriel]], [[concept/asma-al-afuww|the name al-Afuww]], [[concept/tawba|repentance]] and [[passage/quran-13-6|al-Ra'd, verse 6]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both reports are given without chain or collection, and the occasion of the verse is not referenced) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:19:30}
- He speaks of the two recording angels, the angel of good deeds on the right and the angel of bad deeds on the left, and says the angels ask forgiveness for those on earth and are glad when a servant repents, and that the supplication of the angels for a person is answered. See [[concept/malaika|angels]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the account is given by sense without chain or collection and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:21:30}
- He relates that each gate of paradise calls its people, the gate of those who stand in prayer, the gate of jihad, the gate of enjoining good and forbidding evil and the gate of knowledge, and that Abu Bakr asked whether anyone is called from all the gates, and was answered yes. He also says the forerunner of this community enters paradise without reckoning. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[concept/jannah|paradise]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not named and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:24:30}
- He says the more knowledge and worship a servant has, the more fear he has, giving by sense that the Prophet said he is the nearest of them to his Lord yet the most fearful, that if people knew what he knew they would laugh little and weep much, and that there is no space of four fingers in the sky without an angel prostrating. He says the verse describing believers whose hearts shake with fear over what they offer means they pray and fear it will not be accepted, and that a person who sleeps eight hours of every twenty-four spends about twenty years of a sixty-year life asleep. See [[concept/khawf|fear of God]] and [[passage/quran-23-60|al-Mu'minun, verse 60]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the sayings of the Prophet are quoted by sense without narrator or collection, and the figure on years spent asleep is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:32:00}
- He tells of the Hijra, saying Abu Bakr walked sometimes in front of the Prophet, sometimes behind, sometimes on his right and left, out of fear for him, and that in the cave Abu Bakr went in first to look it over, the Prophet slept on Abu Bakr's lap, and a snake bit Abu Bakr until his tears fell on the face of the Prophet. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[place/ghar-thawr|the cave of Thawr]] and [[event/hijra|the Hijra]]. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain or collection and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:29:30}
- He relates that Dawud asked to see the scales and was shown their pans, and the report ends with the mercy of God prevailing; he then tells of one asked how he was saved, by knowledge or deed, who answered that it was by a word. He says the Prophet spoke of words light on the tongue, heavy in the scales and beloved to the Merciful, which he gives as glorifying God, the Almighty. See [[person/dawud|Dawud]] and [[concept/mizan|the scale]]. (needs_source: the report of Dawud and the story of the man are given without a chain, and the saying of the Prophet is quoted by sense without narrator) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:34:00}
- He relates that a Mother of the Believers was sitting in remembrance of God and the Prophet taught her words of praise by the pleasure of God and the ink of His words, and he says the heavens and the earth next to the Footstool are like a ring thrown in a desert, and the Footstool next to the Throne the same. See [[concept/dhikr|remembrance of God]]. (needs_source: the Mother of the Believers is not named, and the narrator and collection of both reports are not given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:35:30}
- He says there is no sun and no moon in the hereafter and no sleep in paradise, and relates the account of a man in paradise who asked his Lord to let him farm; he sows, the crop comes up at once. See [[concept/jannah|paradise]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not named and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:38:30}
- He says by sense that Jesus will come, kill the false Messiah, break the cross and kill the pig and find the Muslims in the mosque, where the imam steps back to let him lead and Jesus says he came as a follower of Muhammad so they should pray behind their imam. Gog and Magog then drink the lake of Tiberias and are destroyed by worms after Jesus and the believers pray, birds carry them away, rain washes the earth, it returns its blessings, and a gentle wind takes the believers so that the Hour stands upon the worst of people. He says these are narrations of the sound collections. See [[concept/dajjal|the false Messiah]], [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]] and [[concept/yajuj-majuj|Gog and Magog]]. (needs_source: the narrator and collection of these end-time narrations are not named, and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:43:00}
- He gives the second reason as being struck by the reputation of the righteous without following their deeds, saying people admire Abu Bakr and Khalid ibn al-Walid in history but what matters is to apply what one reads. He gives the third as wanting knowledge without acting on it, asking of each lesson what one did with it, and says by sense that whoever acts on what he knows, God teaches him what he did not know. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on acting on knowledge is quoted by sense without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:45:00}
- He tells how Musa, asked who was the most knowledgeable, said he was, and God told him of a servant, al-Khidr, at the meeting of the two seas, and that Musa and his servant took a fish with them, forgot it at the rock and it took its way into the sea. He says the Qur'an places every word precisely and that no word could be put in the place of another. See [[person/musa|Musa]], [[person/al-khidr|al-Khidr]], [[passage/quran-18-60|al-Kahf, verse 60]] and [[passage/quran-18-61|al-Kahf, verse 61]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the account of who was most knowledgeable is given by sense without narrator or collection, and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:47:00}
- He observes that the Qur'an gives Zakariyya three days in one place and three nights in another, says that day in the language is daytime and night comes before day, and that Makki surahs, those before the Hijra wherever revealed, mention night first while Madani ones mention day, so Maryam, a Makki surah, speaks of nights and Al Imran, a Madani one, of days. He reads the phrase about al-Khidr as one of God's servants as showing knowledge given directly, and lists the kinds: knowledge taught directly as God taught Adam the names, knowledge for one who acts on what he knows, knowledge by the pen, and knowledge without the pen as in the Prophet, who neither read nor wrote. See [[person/zakariyya|Zakariyya]], [[concept/makki-wa-madani|Makki and Madani revelation]], [[person/adam|Adam]], [[concept/ilm|knowledge]], [[passage/quran-3-41|Al Imran, verse 41]], [[passage/quran-19-10|Maryam, verse 10]], [[passage/quran-2-31|al-Baqara, verse 31]] and [[passage/quran-18-65|al-Kahf, verse 65]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:50:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet advised, at night, closing the doors and covering or tying up vessels, since Satan does not open a closed door. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not named and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:52:00}
- He returns to Musa asking to follow al-Khidr, who says Musa will not be able to bear what he does not grasp, and Musa says he will find him patient if God wills; he says al-Khidr later reminded him he had warned him, and relays the lesson of the people of knowledge that one should not bind oneself to conditions one cannot meet. See [[concept/sabr|patience]], [[passage/quran-18-68|al-Kahf, verse 68]], [[passage/quran-18-69|al-Kahf, verse 69]] and [[passage/quran-18-72|al-Kahf, verse 72]].
