This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:56:30}
- He relates that the Prophet said the vilest name with God is king of kings, since none is king of kings but God, noting that rulers were once called by it. (needs_source: the narrator and collection are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 00:58:00}
- He turns to the world turning toward people while they turn toward it, saying the world flees from one who chases it and follows one who flees it, and giving by sense a saying that the world is told to serve whoever serves God and wear out whoever serves it. He says that every passing day takes one further from the world and nearer the hereafter, as a traveller from Cairo to Alexandria draws nearer Alexandria with each stretch. See [[concept/dunya|the world]] and [[concept/akhira|the hereafter]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about the world serving is quoted by sense without a chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:01:30}
- He tells a report of Jesus and a companion who had loaves, one of which went missing, and a division into three portions, one for Jesus, one for the companion and one for whoever ate the loaf, to which the man answered that he had, and he draws the lesson that this is the world and its people. See [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]] and [[concept/dunya|the world]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain or source and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:05:30}
- He says these are the six reasons that close the door of success on servants walking the path to God, and that he will go on to the remaining branches of faith in Ibn al-Qayyim's explanation of the hadith, beginning with the works of the tongue, the first being tawhid; he says that whoever pronounces tawhid is judged by his tongue unless he denies a matter of God's religion. See [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]], [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]] and [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:07:30}
- He defines the Qur'an as the speech of God, eternal, miraculous in its style, worshipped by reciting it, sent down on the heart of the Prophet in parts over about twenty years, and refers to the verse about it being sent down on a mountain. He says the least a Muslim should do is finish it once a month, that whoever does not has abandoned it, and relates that Ibn Umar finished it every Friday and then began again, gathering children to say amin behind him; he also gives by sense a saying of the Prophet that the best deed is that of one who arrives and departs, reading from start to end and starting again. See [[practice/khatm-al-quran|completing the Qur'an]], [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] and [[passage/quran-59-21|al-Hashr, verse 21]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the practice of Ibn Umar is told without a chain, and the saying of the Prophet is quoted by sense without narrator or collection, and the span of about twenty years is a figure given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:09:30}
- He says one earns reward for reciting with or without understanding, relating by sense that each letter earns a reward, the opening letters of a surah counted letter by letter, and that whoever recites much has light in his tongue, eyes, ears, heart and grave and on the bridge. He says the degrees of paradise match the verses of the Qur'an, and that on the Day of Resurrection the parents of a child who memorised it are clothed by angels. He advises memorising five verses a day and reciting them in prayer, so that the whole Qur'an might be memorised in three or four years, and says obedience, piety, lawful food and a tongue busy with remembrance help it. See [[practice/hifz-al-quran|memorising the Qur'an]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the sayings on letters, light, degrees and the parents are quoted by sense without chain or collection, and the three to four years for memorising is a figure given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:12:00}
- He tells that al-Shafii, sitting in the circle of Malik, turned the pages gently so as not to disturb him and sipped water out of reverence, and that Malik told people that whoever wants knowledge should go to al-Shafii. See [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafii]] and [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik ibn Anas]]. (needs_source: the reports are told without a chain or source and the recording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:14:00}
- He tells a parable of a lion cub raised among sheep who thinks he is a sheep until a real lion shows him his reflection, and says the lion is the Muslim who forgot his Islam. He says the Companions would enter on kings and speak with the Book and the Sunna, wanting nothing from worldly people but belief in God. This is his view. (needs_source: the parable is told without a source and the remark on the Companions is a general statement) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep18 01:17:30}

## Episode 19: the branches of faith in bodily deeds, beginning with purity
- He opens by saying this is a lesson in his explanation of Fath al-Bari, and recalls the hadith that faith has many branches and that modesty is a branch of faith, saying some deeds may seem unworthy of being branches of faith. See [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]] and [[concept/iman|faith]]. (needs_source: the narrator and the collection of the hadith are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:02:30}
- He tells of the mosque of harm, saying revelation came to warn the Prophet that it was made to harm Muslims and divide them, and that a mosque founded on piety from the first day is worthier of being stood in. See [[passage/quran-9-107|al-Tawba, verse 107]], [[passage/quran-9-108|al-Tawba, verse 108]] and [[event/masjid-al-dirar-builders-ask-the-prophet-to-pray|the builders of the mosque of harm]]. This is his account. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:05:30}
- He relates that Umar said people were dealt with in the Prophet's time while revelation was coming down, and that now they are judged by what they show, trusting whoever shows good. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the saying of Umar is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:07:00}
- He relates that someone looked at the Kaaba and said how great and how beautiful it is, but that the sanctity of the believer with God is greater than its sanctity. (needs_source: the narrator is garbled in the transcript and no chain is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:06:30}
- He relates a divine saying that God created the servant for Himself so he should not play, took charge of his provision so he should not tire himself, that whoever finds God finds everything and whoever loses Him finds nothing, and that He is dearer to the servant than all things. He says whoever finds God has lost nothing. This is his view. (needs_source: the divine saying is given without a chain and its narrator is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:20:30}
- He says people feel shame before a father, a brother, a son or a wife but not before God, and that people obey the commands of others yet play with God's commands, citing that it is greatly hateful to God to say what one does not do. He says people were created to worship, mentioning by sense that people are asleep and wake only when they die, and regret then does not help. See [[concept/haya|modesty]], [[passage/quran-61-3|al-Saff, verse 3]] and [[passage/quran-51-56|al-Dhariyat, verse 56]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying that people are asleep is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:11:00}
- He says provision is guaranteed by God, that ease in one's work and earnings comes from God's favour, and that relying on the Creator is what a believer should do. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:14:00}
- He says people give their care to worldly affairs but when it is about religion they hold back, and urges reliance on God, saying deeds people think small may be great with God. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:21:30}
- He says a person should not despair of God's mercy however many his sins, citing that God forgives all sins, and mentions by sense a saying of the Prophet on being freed of sins. He then relates that a man came to the Prophet saying he had relations with his wife in a day of Ramadan, and that the Prophet told him of fasting sixty days or feeding sixty poor people, which he presents as the Prophet's mercy. See [[passage/quran-39-53|al-Zumar, verse 53]] and [[concept/tawba|repentance]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying and the report of the man are given without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:32:00}
- He mentions by sense that the Prophet told his companions not to insult Abu Jahl in front of his son, who had come as a Muslim, because insulting the dead hurts the living, and that when companions cursed a man who drank wine the Prophet told them not to, as he loves God and His Messenger. He says this opens the door of mercy to people, who differ in their trials, and one should be with a brother against Satan rather than for Satan against him. See [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both reports are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:33:30}
- He relates that the Prophet joked but said only what is true, and that a man asked to be carried and the Prophet said he would carry him on the offspring of a camel. He says a word may bring harm, and mentions by sense that a word spoken with nothing behind it is recorded with God as a lie. (needs_source: the report and the saying on the recorded lie are given without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:37:00}
- He turns to the subject of the hadith, saying the bodily deeds of faith number thirty-eight branches and that the first is purity. He says purity is of two kinds, physical and spiritual, and impurity is of two kinds, the minor and the major. See [[concept/tahara|purity]] and [[concept/hadath-impurity|ritual impurity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the number of branches is stated without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:43:00}
- He says long nails gather dirt and cites a report about Gabriel's delay and dirt under the nails, and says nail polish does not suit prayer. See [[person/jibril|Gabriel]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on Gabriel's delay is given without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:52:00}
- He says the minor impurity is what comes out of either passage, and that ablution is not broken unless he hears a sound or smells a wind, or urine or stool comes out. See [[practice/wudu|ablution]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on sound or smell is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:44:00}
- He says Abu Hanifa holds that laughing in prayer breaks ablution, reasoning that one stands in prayer before God with the manners owed to a great king. See [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]]. (needs_source: his account of Abu Hanifa's view has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:44:30}
- He says one enters the toilet with the left foot, and that the mosque is entered with the right foot and left with the left, giving the supplication of mercy on entering the mosque and of favour on leaving, and mentions by sense that Satan and his tribe see people from where they do not see them. This is his view. (needs_source: the supplications and the saying on Satan are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:47:00}
- He advises a person troubled by doubts about his ablution not to repeat it but to carry on and defy Satan, and says ablution may be done in the bathroom provided God's name is not said inside. See [[practice/wudu|ablution]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:50:00}
- He describes the Prophet's ablution as reported, pouring water over his hands three times, passing water between the fingers and moving a ring, rinsing mouth and nose three times and washing the face three times, and mentions by sense that the Prophet said this is the ablution of the prophets before him and whoever adds or subtracts has erred and done wrong. See [[practice/wudu|ablution]]. (needs_source: the narrator and the collection are not named and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:57:00}
- He says the major impurity comes from intercourse or a wet dream, and describes washing, which is to remove impurity, do the ablution of prayer, pour water over the head three times and rub the scalp, then wash the right side and the left, calling this the Prophet's model. He says water for it may come from the sky, river, spring, well or tap. See [[practice/ghusl-al-janaba|the major ablution]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:02:00}
- He says that if the imam's ablution breaks in prayer he asks one of those behind him in the first row to take his place, as a teacher taught him, and mentions by sense the saying that those of maturity should stand close to the Prophet. This is his view. (needs_source: the ruling from his teacher and the saying are given without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:08:00}
- On menstruation he says that if a woman's bleeding stops just before the call to the sunset prayer she must bathe and pray. See [[concept/hayd-menstruation|menstruation]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:10:30}
- He says the purity that matters more is purity of the heart, not of the face or the clothes, clearing it of vices, and adds that one should keep his prayer clothing free of impurity, and need not ask about water found at a place that looks clean. See [[concept/tahara|purity]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:11:30}
- He says that in prayer a woman need show only her face and hands, that some jurists hold the foot to be part of what must be covered so a covering such as socks is needed, and that a man too must keep the awra covered so his prayer is not lost. See [[concept/awra|awra]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the jurists who treat the foot as awra are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:16:00}
- He compares the obligatory and voluntary prayers to a house with a garden, so a thief has to pass the garden first, and mentions by sense the divine saying that the servant keeps drawing near to God with voluntary acts until God loves him. See [[practice/salah|prayer]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the narrator and the collection of the divine saying are not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:17:00}
- He mentions hajj and umra, and mentions by sense a warning that one who is able to perform hajj and does not may die as a Jew, a Magian or a Christian. See [[practice/hajj|hajj]]. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:18:00}
- On zakat he says it is two and a half in every hundred, and that one with a business keeps accounts and works out his zakat at the end of the year. He gives his own fatwa that a low-paid employee with dependents who pays rent may take zakat, saying he will answer for it before God. See [[practice/zakat|zakat]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the rate is stated without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:19:00}
- He says generosity is a human quality and mentions by sense that the miser does not enter Paradise and that faith and miserliness do not join in a believer's heart. See [[concept/karam|generosity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both sayings are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:23:00}
- He says generosity need not be lavish, mentions by sense the saying to gather over your food and it will be blessed, and relates that Jabir ibn Abdullah invited Companions to a meal and the Prophet had the food served in groups of ten, and it did not run out, then told them to leave the rest for their households. See [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying and the report of Jabir's meal are given without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:25:00}
- He lists what falls under generosity as feeding people and honouring the guest, then voluntary fasting and seclusion in the mosque, and then fleeing with one's religion, and says he will take that last branch up next Thursday so that young people do not misunderstand it. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep19 01:26:00}

## Episode 20: fleeing with one's religion
- He recalls that last Monday's lesson was on the branches of faith and says the next branch is fleeing with one's religion; he says Ibn Hajar takes it up in explaining the hadith that faith has sixty branches and that modesty is one of them. See [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]]. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense, without chain or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:02:30}
- He says the jurists divide the world into two, the abode of Islam and the abode of war. He describes the abode of Islam as a land with a large Muslim majority governed by God's law, with mosques, zakat distributed, mercy for the poor and no acceptance of indecency. He describes the abode of war as a land with a Muslim minority that is overpowered, or none at all. He adds that a state bound to Muslims by a covenant of mutual non-aggression stands in a different position, and moves to another one if it breaks the covenant. See [[concept/dar-al-islam|Dar al-Islam]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the division is attributed to the jurists without naming any of them) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:05:00}
- He says he wants strong, successful Muslim professionals who know God: an engineer, a physician who gives two days a week in clinics for the poor, and a lawyer who defends the wronged seeking God's pleasure. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:17:30}
- He says the believer moves among five kinds of light: his speech, his action, his gaze, what enters him and what leaves him, all leading to paradise. He then relates by sense a divine saying (hadith qudsi) that the servant keeps drawing near to God with voluntary acts until God loves him, and then God becomes the hearing, sight, hand and foot of the servant, and answers him if he calls. This is his view. (needs_source: the divine saying is given by sense, without narrator or collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:19:30}
- He says the opposite is five kinds of darkness: speech made of backbiting, tale-bearing, attacks on people's honour, lying and false witness; a gaze toward what God forbade; hearing only evil; and movement only toward evil, like a bat that flies only at night. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:20:00}
- He says the Prophet used to address delegations from the tribes in their own dialect, and he gives as an example a saying pronounced in such a dialect, quoting the Prophet as saying he spoke to them at the level of their minds because his Lord commanded it. The wording of the example is garbled in the transcript. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without chain, and the example wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:26:00}
- He says God makes use of those who reject faith to serve His book, and gives as an example Germans who applied their skill to printing the Qur'an on good paper in clear letters. This is his view. (needs_source: the example names no press and gives no date) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:29:00}
- Turning to the branch itself, he says that if a person sees a land full of every evil, with usury, open wine, killing, no penalties applied and honour violated, it is a place a person should leave. He then gives the other view he holds, resting on the unrestricted public interest (al-maslaha al-mursala): there is no better place than the one where a person can live uprightly, and it should not be left to those who do not fear God. He holds that Muslims should fill every field, and that when the people of evil set up a venue the people of good should set up one too. He mentions that some scholars say a Muslim may work in a conventional bank in order to learn and later run banks, and says he has reservations about that opinion. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:31:30}
- He relates a saying of a Companion whose name is garbled in the transcript, that three things made him laugh and three made him weep. As he gives them, the first three are one who hopes for this world while death is seeking him, one who is heedless though he is not left unwatched, and one who laughs broadly without knowing whether his Lord is pleased or angry with him. The wording of the first two is partly garbled. (needs_source: a Companion's saying without chain, the name is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:42:30}
- He asks whether anyone has told the listener that he will die, cross the bridge over the Fire and be saved, and says the heart has two voices, one wailing for the afterlife and one for this world. He says that when a person prefers the deeds of the afterlife this world recedes, and the reverse, and that this world is darkness and the afterlife is light, which cannot dwell together. He adds that the heart is a place for good deeds that Satan wants to enter, and that the person must block him step by step. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:38:00}
- He continues the Companion's saying with the three that made him weep: parting from the beloved ones; the terror of the great day, for which he recites a verse about the day of the Cry; and standing before God. See [[passage/quran-50-41|Qaf, verse 41]]. (needs_source: a Companion's saying without chain, partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:43:30}
- He cites the words of Satan to those who followed him, that he had no authority over them except to call and that they answered, so they should not blame him. See [[passage/quran-14-22|Ibrahim, verse 22]]. He adds that through true faith a person overcomes any force. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:46:00}
- He says that if a person sees a land where sins are committed and cannot live in it alone, he should migrate to a land that brings him nearer to God and to faith. He then says that living among people and bearing their trials is better than withdrawing from them, because bearing them brings merit, steadfastness and striving. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:46:30}
- He relates by sense that God loves to see the trace of His blessing on His servant, and tells that Abu Hanifa had a wide house, had a very large silk business in Iraq, and divided his income into two halves, one for his household and one for God. He says that God tests the miser. See [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] and [[concept/al-bukhl|miserliness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense, and the story of Abu Hanifa has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:50:00}
- He says by sense that the miser and the dayyuth do not enter paradise, and asks who the dayyuth is. He answers, as he gives the Prophet's words, that he is the man who knows of indecency in his family and lets it pass, and calls on men to show protective care for their households. He also relates by sense that wrongdoing with a neighbour's wife is graver than with other women, and that the adulterer with his neighbour's wife is among those whom God will not look at on the Day of Resurrection. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadiths are given by sense without chain, and the wording is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:53:30}
- He says fulfilling what one has bound oneself to is part of the believer's character: God did not impose the vow, the person imposed it on himself, so he must fulfil it, and he must keep faith with people too. On expiation he says one who swears an oath and breaks it must feed ten poor people or fast three consecutive days, and one who broke a Ramadan fast must fast sixty consecutive days, restarting if the run is broken, or feed sixty poor people. See [[practice/kaffarat-al-yamin|the expiation for oaths]], [[concept/kaffara|expiation]] and [[practice/sawm-ramadan|fasting in Ramadan]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the rulings and numbers are his own, with no evidence given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:56:00}
- He relates that al-Hasan said that when a calamity strikes him he praises God for four things, the first being that it is not in his religion. He says a calamity in religion is the hardest and asks God to keep calamity out of his religion, then thanks God that the calamity was not greater. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is attributed to al-Hasan without chain, and only the first of four things is given clearly) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 00:58:30}
- He says a believer must always be moving on God's path and be a good example for Muslims, and prays that God make the believers leaders. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:02:30}
- He turns to the next branch, as he names it chastity through marriage, which he says concerns the young in particular and weighs on him because of economic hardship. He says people postpone marriage even in a crisis, and recalls that in the 1970s there were weddings at nearly every gathering, whereas now a few take place each year. See [[practice/nikah|Marriage]]. This is his view. (needs_source: a personal recollection with no count or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:03:00}
- He cites a saying of Ibn Mas'ud in this context, whose wording is garbled in the transcript. He says marriage should not be treated as a feasibility study of profit and loss, since the one who provides is God, and that they honoured what is lawful so that what is unlawful was rare, and who "they" are is unclear in the transcript. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abd Allah ibn Mas'ud]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Mas'ud has no reference and its wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:04:30}
- He criticises the costly conditions families set on a suitor, such as a flat, jewellery and a wedding hall, and says they lead young men to postpone or give up marrying. He says parents share the responsibility, as does the media. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:09:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet asked a man whom he calls by a garbled name, apparently Ukkaf whether he was married and whether he could afford to marry, and that on hearing that he could, the Prophet criticised in strong terms staying unmarried when able; the wording is garbled. He then says, by sense, that God has taken it upon Himself to help the one who marries seeking chastity, that he trusts God's promise, and he urges young men to rely on God rather than hold out for a guaranteed job. See [[practice/nikah|Marriage]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadiths are given by sense without chain and the first is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:12:30}
- He quotes a line, without naming its author, that the true orphan is not the child whose parent has died but the one whose mother or father is too busy for him. He says parents should attend to their children's rights from the start, choosing a good name and a good mother. This is his view. (needs_source: the quoted line has no author or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:14:00}
- He says parents should teach their children the Qur'an: the family can memorise ten verses together, each member reciting in turn, and a parent should sit with the child and read with him. He tells of a household he knows where the children recite many surahs and apply the rules of recitation. See [[practice/hifz-al-quran|Memorising the Qur'an]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the household is not named and the story has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep20 01:16:30}

## Episode 21: the parable of guidance and knowledge like abundant rain
- He opens by speaking about the tremor that had just happened, saying it comes suddenly, that it is one of the signs God shows in the horizons and in people themselves, and he recites the sura named for the earthquake and calls the listeners to repent and return to God. See [[passage/quran-99-1|al-Zalzala, verse 1]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:16:00}
- He says there is no refuge in the street, on the stairs, in a grave, on a mountain or in the desert, and that safety from what God sends comes only through fear of God. See [[concept/taqwa|God-consciousness]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:07:30}
- He says the ancient Italians had a god of fire and ironwork, and that the word volcano comes from his name. (needs_source: the claim about Italian beliefs and the word origin is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:10:00}
- He says the complex instruments built to measure earthquakes cannot predict one even seconds before it happens, so that knowledge stays with God and human reason stays in need of Him, and he adds that dogs are reported to bark in an unusual way shortly before a quake. This is his view. (needs_source: the report about dogs is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:11:00}
- He recalls that Umar stopped at the border of al-Sham when told that plague had broken out there, and that he turned back after hearing the Prophet's saying that one should not enter a land where plague is, nor leave it if one is already in it, saying that they were fleeing from God's decree to God's decree. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], [[event/plague-of-amwas|the plague of Amwas]] and [[concept/al-taun|plague]]. (needs_source: the report and the saying are given by sense without a chain, and the person who relates the saying is not clearly named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:15:00}
- Announcing the twenty-first lesson of his explanation of the hadiths of Sahih al-Bukhari, he relates from Abu Musa al-Ash'ari that the Prophet said the guidance and knowledge God sent him with is like abundant rain that fell on land: part of it was good soil that took the water and grew much grass, part was hard ground that held the water so that God benefited people, who drank, watered and sowed, and part was barren flats that neither held water nor grew plants. He says that is the likeness of one who gained understanding of God's religion and benefited from what the Prophet brought, learned and taught, and of one who did not lift his head to it and did not accept God's guidance. See [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]], [[concept/rain|rain]] and [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:17:30}
- He explains that rain falls on one of three kinds of land: fertile land that grows plants and herbage, hard land that holds the water so that people draw it, and flat barren land that neither grows nor holds anything, and he says the first two bring benefit. He adds that water is what gives life, and that where there is water there is life, so knowledge is like that water. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:19:00}
- He says the wind is one of God's soldiers and gives speeds in miles per hour at which it begins to be felt on the face, to move small flags and to bring down chimneys and trees, saying that above a certain speed it uproots houses with those inside. See [[concept/wind|wind]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the wind-speed figures are given without a reference and are garbled in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:24:30}
- He recalls the Battle of the Trench, saying the Jews on the rear side broke their pact with the Prophet, and that on a bitterly cold night the Prophet asked for a man to bring news of the enemy. He says the man sent saw Abu Sufyan pass close in front of him, held back remembering the Prophet's instruction, and that the wind then scattered the besiegers, so that Abu Sufyan and those with him withdrew, which he counts as another of God's soldiers. See [[event/battle-of-the-trench|the Battle of the Trench]] and [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan ibn Harb]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain, the man sent is not clearly named, and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:35:30}
- He tells of the army that came against the Kaaba, saying its soldiers took camels belonging to a man who came and asked their leader for them, and the man answered that the camels were his and that the House has its Lord, and that birds came carrying stones, each stone marked for the one it would strike, and left the army like eaten straw, as another soldier of God. See [[event/year-of-the-elephant-and-birth|the Year of the Elephant]] and [[passage/quran-105-5|al-Fil, verse 5]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the exchange about the camels is given without a chain, and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:42:30}
- He says God favors no race over another and no colour over another, and that nearness and distance are measured by nearness to God. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:43:00}
- He recounts the visit of the three guests to Abraham, saying Abraham was frightened and they told him not to fear, that they had been sent to the people of Lut, and he touches on Abraham's etiquette in receiving guests. See [[person/ibrahim|Abraham]] and [[person/lut|Lut]]. (needs_source: the account is given without a reference, and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:45:30}
- He says God made iron stronger than rock, fire that melts iron, and water that puts out fire, and that every living thing is made from water, human beings included. See [[passage/quran-21-30|al-Anbiya, verse 30]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:52:00}
- He says that a person who falls ill tends to turn back to God from the first moment of sickness, and that the illness is itself a favour that reminds him of God. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:52:30}
- He returns to the hadith and says that, just as rain revives the earth and it comes to life with greenery after the water reaches it, the guidance and knowledge the Prophet brought give life. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:53:30}
- He says the heart is like a part of the earth and that knowledge descends on it, so that a dead heart is brought to life by God's leave, and that hearts are of three kinds. The first kind takes the knowledge and applies it, as Umar did, and he stresses that knowledge must be turned into action. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 00:58:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet warned of a man reclining on his couch who, when a command of the Prophet reaches him, says that he will follow only what he finds in God's book, and he says the Prophet's forbidding is like God's forbidding. See [[concept/hujjiyyat-al-sunna|the authority of the Sunna]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain or collection, and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:08:30}
- He answers by pointing to two verses, one that makes following the Prophet a way to God's love and one that tells believers to take what the Messenger gives them, and he says the Prophet received the Book and the like of it with it, meaning wisdom, so that the Sunna is also revelation. See [[passage/quran-3-31|Al Imran, verse 31]], [[passage/quran-59-7|al-Hashr, verse 7]] and [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying about the Book and its like is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:10:00}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet asked how it will be with the people when they no longer command what is right and forbid what is wrong, and he links this to commanding good and forbidding wrong in society. See [[concept/amr-bil-maruf|commanding the right]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain, and the recording is garbled in places) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep21 01:13:30}

## Episode 22: the Muslim is one from whose tongue and hand Muslims are safe
- He opens the tenth hadith of Sahih al-Bukhari, in the Book of Faith, recalling that the previous lesson ended the hadith that faith has some seventy branches and modesty is a branch of faith, and says the book shows faith to be to the believer what the spirit is to the body. See [[concept/iman|faith]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:01:30}
- He says a person of weak faith has no modesty and no good is hoped from him, and relates by sense that the Prophet found a man rebuking his brother over modesty and told him to leave him, for modesty is part of faith. See [[concept/haya|modesty]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:03:00}
- He says lawful earnings come in degrees and unlawful ones do too, and prays for clean lawful provision morning and evening with blessing in it even if little. See [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:10:00}
- He mentions by sense the hadith that if you did not sin God would take you away and bring a people who sin, ask forgiveness and are forgiven, and says all of us err. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:11:00}
- He relates that a Companion, feeling beside the Prophet as if soaring with him but losing that feeling at home among wives, children and work, called himself a hypocrite to Abu Bakr, who said he felt the same. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain and the transcript is badly garbled, so the Companion is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:12:30}
- He speaks of the Prophet's mercy and ease toward Muslims, relating that he prayed with the Companions at night for some nights, then entered his room and did not come out. See [[practice/qiyam-al-layl|night prayer]]. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:13:30}
- He relates that a man asked the Prophet whether it was every year, that the Prophet stayed silent and the man asked three times, and that he then said had he said yes it would have become obligatory; he cites the verse telling believers not to ask about things which, if shown, would trouble them, and speaks against one who presses with questions until something is forbidden because of them, in a garbled stretch. See [[passage/quran-5-101|al-Ma'ida, verse 101]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted without a chain and does not name what was asked about) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:14:30}
- He mentions by sense that this religion is firm, so one should go into it gently. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:16:00}
- He says the Companions asked the Prophet about only some thirty matters, and, discussing questions about food and marriage, that what is forbidden is known from what is known of the religion by necessity, giving pork and anything made from it, and the milk kin forbidden in marriage, as examples. This is his view. (needs_source: his figure of thirty questions has no reference and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:27:00}
- He reads the hadith with its chain, which the transcript renders only in part, reaching Abdullah ibn Amr, and relates from him that the Prophet said the Muslim is the one from whose tongue and hand Muslims are safe, and the emigrant is the one who abandons what God forbade. He says all the troubles come from Islam's absence from the believer's heart. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-amr-ibn-al-as|Abdullah ibn Amr]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the chain is garbled in the transcript and only the last narrator is clear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:28:00}
- He says the stress laid on the tongue shows it holds major sins, of which he names backbiting and tale-bearing, lying and false witness, and a fourth that the transcript renders unclearly. See [[concept/namimah|tale-bearing]], [[concept/kadhib|lying]] and [[concept/false-testimony-zur|false witness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: his count of four major sins of the tongue is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:28:30}
- He relates a saying that one busied himself with mending his own faults and so was kept from the faults of others, and urges each person to busy himself with his own faults. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given without a name or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:29:00}
- He mentions by sense that many who fast gain only hunger and thirst and many who pray at night gain only wakefulness, and goes on to backbiting and tale-bearing. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:31:00}
- He says lying is the second major sin of the tongue, that we lie in words and in deeds, and that the liar lies in the heart and the mouth. He relates by sense a report of a man whose jaw is torn with hooks of fire until they reach his ears, said to be one of the Prophet's community who tells a lie that spreads far, and advises not to repeat everything one hears. See [[concept/kadhib|lying]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report is quoted by sense without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:39:30}
- He says false witness is the third major sin of the tongue, relating by sense that the Prophet kept repeating the warning against false speech until the Companions wished he would stop, and says whoever took a right by false testimony must return it to its owners or his repentance is not complete. See [[concept/false-testimony-zur|false witness]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:41:00}
- He says the scholars named four ways to know one's faults: learning from a teacher who knows the human soul, a sincere friend as a mirror, one's enemies, whose criticism he puts at about a tenth true, a figure given without a source, and dealing with people. He relates a saying asking God's mercy on one who gifts us our faults, says one should want the truth to appear on another's tongue rather than one's own, and that disputation breeds rancour. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is given without a name or chain, his tenth-true figure has no reference, and the stretch is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:45:30}
- He says innovation concerns worship, not customs and dress such as trousers, a shirt or a robe, and tells of a gathering where macaroni was called an innovation because the Prophet did not eat it, offering it as an example of misusing the term. See [[concept/bida|innovation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the account has no names or place) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:45:00}
- He says Islam does not permit isolation or withdrawal to mountains and deserts, that it is family and community, a Muslim community from the east of China to Andalusia to South Africa, and that Muslims should engage society and be a model. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:49:30}
- He relates from Ibn Masud that a time came when innovations spread until they were taken for the Sunna, so that whoever tried to change an innovation was said to be changing the Sunna, and gives birthday celebrations as an example of such new customs. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Masud]] and [[concept/bida|innovation]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of Ibn Masud is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:55:30}
- He says whoever took wrongful money should return it to its owners, that lawful and unlawful are hard to tell apart in modern work, and advises giving doubtful gains to the poor. He relates that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz covered his nose against a scent, saying the Muslims benefit from it only by its smell. See [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] and [[concept/halal-haram|the lawful and the unlawful]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz is given without a chain and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 00:59:30}
- He explains the second half of the hadith: the emigration from Mecca to Medina ended, as the Prophet is reported by sense to have said there is no emigration after the conquest but jihad and intention, so today one emigrates by abandoning what God forbade, and whoever does so is reckoned with the emigrants in rank. See [[event/hijra|the Hijra]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the hadith is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:00:30}
- He reports a judgment that of two men, one whom sin never tempted and one tempted who abstained, the second is better. This is his view. (needs_source: the judgment is given without a name or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:01:00}
- He relates by sense a saying he ascribes to the Prophet that the world is the home of one who has no home and the wealth of one who has no wealth, gathered for by one without sense, and adds that the world is a field for the believer and that each builds his home before death. See [[concept/dunya|the world]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:04:30}
- He relates from Ibn Hajar that the hadith is broader for the tongue than the hand, since the tongue speaks of the past, present and future and the hand takes part only through writing, and that emigration is of two kinds, a hidden one, leaving what the base self urges, and an open one, fleeing trials to be with the sincere believers. See [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]] and [[event/hijra|the Hijra]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the attribution to Ibn Hajar is given without a work or page) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:06:30}
- He says Umar used to say they left nine tenths of the lawful for fear of falling into the unlawful, and that Umar feared being told by his Lord that he had spent his good things in worldly life; he cites the verse about being asked on that Day about delights, glossing them as cold water in summer. See [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[passage/quran-102-8|al-Takathur, verse 8]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the reports of Umar are given without a chain and the figure of nine tenths is rendered unclearly) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:07:30}
- He says one should thank God for the blessings of rest, security and the ability to read and write, and urges young people to gather three or four people and teach them reading and writing. He relates by sense that the Prophet had captives teach ten Muslims reading and writing (the transcript does not name Badr), and mentions that the scholar's ink is weighed against the blood of martyrs, in a stretch that is partly garbled. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. (needs_source: both reports are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep22 01:09:00}

## Episode 23: the best Islam is feeding food and greeting those you know and those you do not
- He relates from Abdullah ibn Umar, as the twelfth hadith of al-Bukhari's collection, that a man asked the Prophet which Islam is best and he answered feeding food and greeting those you know and those you do not know, mentions Ibn Hajar's commentary on al-Bukhari, and says the Prophet's words were few but gathered much. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]], [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]] and [[practice/greeting-salam|greeting]]. (needs_source: the text has no reference to the place of narration) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:01:30}
- He says the Prophet was skilled at assemblies of knowledge, quotes a Companion by sense that sitting in one assembly of knowledge is better for him than a hundred prayer cycles, says mercy descends most on seekers of knowledge so that a question benefits the asker, the answering scholar and the listener, and cites the saying to be a scholar or a learner and at the least not to dislike them. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the Companion's saying and the saying on being a scholar or a learner have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:03:00}
- He says some people turn away from scholars because they only say what is forbidden, lawful and wrong, and that this should not put anyone off the people of knowledge, and he relates a saying he attributes to a scholar called Abu Hamid that scholars are more merciful to people than their fathers and mothers, since parents try to keep a child from the fire of this world and scholars from the fire of the hereafter. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying attributed to Abu Hamid has no chain and the name is not clear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:05:00}
- He relates that Ibn Abbas, seeking knowledge, would wait at a scholar's door while the wind blew dust over him, and that when Zayd ibn Thabit went to mount his horse Ibn Abbas held it for him, saying this is how they were told to treat scholars, and Zayd got down and kissed Ibn Abbas's hand, saying they were told to honour the family of their Prophet. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]] and [[person/zayd-ibn-thabit|Zayd ibn Thabit]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:06:30}
- He relates by sense that the Prophet said he saw in a dream that he drew water from a well, that Abu Bakr drew a bucket or two, and that Umar then drew until the bucket became great and the people were watered, and says scholars took this to refer to knowledge. He draws from it that the Prophet knew the gifts of his Companions. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the dream is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:08:00}
- He says the Prophet knew his Companions' gifts and gave command to Zayd ibn Haritha and later to the young Usama ibn Zayd, with Abu Bakr and Umar under his command, and relates that when Abu Bakr became caliph he walked beside Usama's mount, Usama offered either to dismount or to let him ride, Abu Bakr refused both, and he asked Usama's leave to keep Umar with him. See [[person/zayd-ibn-haritha|Zayd ibn Haritha]], [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama ibn Zayd]], [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]. (needs_source: the report of Usama and Abu Bakr has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:10:00}
- He says the Companions gave in charity what they loved, citing the verse that righteousness is not attained until one spends of what one loves, and tells of Abu al-Dahdah and a dispute over a palm tree, with the Prophet quoted as saying how many laden clusters and wide houses in Paradise belong to Abu al-Dahdah. See [[passage/quran-3-92|Al Imran, verse 92]] and [[practice/sadaqa|charity]]. (needs_source: the story of Abu al-Dahdah has no chain and the Prophet's saying is quoted by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:16:30}
- He says knowledge has to become action, giving the example of hearing a lesson that cleanliness is part of faith and then going out together to clean the neighbourhood, and says that is where the value of knowledge shows. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:19:30}
- He says faith can wear thin and needs warmth, so a person should attend assemblies of knowledge often to be reminded, and mentions by sense the saying that when you pass the gardens of Paradise you should graze, which the Prophet explained as the assembly of knowledge. See [[concept/ilm|knowledge]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on the gardens of Paradise is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:23:30}
- He says a Muslim does ordinary things such as eating and sleeping with sincerity, and notes that doctors advise about eight hours of sleep, which takes a great share of a lifetime, so it matters to give it a good intention. See [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the number of sleep hours attributed to doctors has no reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:25:30}
- He relates that Abu Bakr was brought food, and when he learned that it came from its giver's fortune-telling for people in the time of ignorance he put his finger in his throat and vomited it, saying it would have come out even with his life, and he mentions by sense that flesh grown from what is forbidden is more fitting for the Fire. He says a person should seek the lawful. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report has no chain and the saying on flesh is quoted by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:31:30}
- He mentions by sense that when a person mentions God's name Satan has no share of the meal, and says to say God's name before eating, to eat with the right hand and from what is in front of you, and to say it at the beginning and the end. See [[practice/adab-al-akl|etiquette of eating]] and [[practice/dua-when-food-is-served|supplication when food is served]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the report on Satan is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:34:30}
- He holds that a Muslim invited to a wedding feast should go, unless he knows the feast contains something sinful. See [[practice/walima|wedding feast]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:38:00}
- He says that when a person eats with the intention of gaining strength for obedience, the meal earns reward, and that sleeping to rest for the duties of the day is itself worship, reminding listeners of the hadith on actions by intentions from the early lessons of the series, and advising an intention for every act. See [[concept/niyya|intention]]. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:47:30}
- He relates from Abdullah ibn Umar, citing a Sunan collection he does not make clear, that among the signs of the Hour is that greeting is given only to those one knows. See [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]] and [[practice/greeting-salam|greeting]]. (needs_source: the collection is not clear and the text is quoted by sense) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:47:30}
- He relates that Jibril told the Prophet to convey her Lord's greeting and his own to Khadija, and that Khadija answered that God is Peace, from Him is peace, and on Jibril be peace. See [[person/jibril|Jibril]] and [[person/khadija|Khadija]]. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:51:30}
- He mentions by sense the Prophet's call to spread greeting among yourselves, and says one should greet those one knows and those one does not, and greet whoever one meets. See [[practice/greeting-salam|greeting]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:52:30}
- He says the greeting is the Muslims' slogan and should replace other salutations, and mentions by sense that of two people who meet the better is the one who begins with the greeting. See [[practice/greeting-salam|greeting]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the saying on beginning the greeting is quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:54:30}
- He mentions by sense that a man entering his house should enter with the right foot, mention God's name and greet his family, and recalls the saying that the best of you is the best to his family. See [[practice/dua-on-entering-the-house|supplication on entering the house]] and [[practice/greeting-salam|greeting]]. This is his view. (needs_source: the sayings are quoted by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:56:00}
- In a second talk later in the recording he relates from Abu Bakr that the Prophet said truth goes with righteousness and both are in Paradise, while lying goes with wickedness and both are in the Fire, that after certainty nothing better is given than well-being, and not to envy, hate or turn away from one another but to be brothers. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] and [[concept/hasad|envy]]. (needs_source: the collection is not named and the chain is only partly audible) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:17:30}
- He says some European scholars, whom he does not name, have written that had Muhammad not been a messenger his character would still call for such a mission, and holds that greatness in the Prophet is found in every aspect, unlike figures great in one respect only. This is his view. (needs_source: the European scholars are not named and no reference is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy sharh-saheeh-al-bukhare/ep23 01:01:00}
